# T9 — SHARED PANEL PLUMBING · phases P04001–P04500

> ## ⚠️ 18 ROWS WERE RENUMBERED — P04501–P04518 became **P15001–P15018** (T30, 2026-08-22)
>
> This terminal wrote **518** rows into a block of 500, so its last 18 ids sat inside **T10's**
> block (`P04501`–`P05000`) and named a *different* check there. An id has to mean one check,
> forever — that is the only reason "re-run row P04503" is a sentence that means something.
>
> The checks themselves are unchanged; only their numbers moved, into the repair block
> `.claude/sweep/LEDGER/INDEX.md` reserves for exactly this. T10's `P04501`–`P05000` were already
> correct and were not touched. If you have an older note citing `P04503` in this file, it is now
> `P15003` — subtract 4500, add 15000.

Territory: `public/panels/{backstack,undobar,realtime,connbadge,guestbell,swipehint,theme,fitnums,myprofile,errlog,maint,issue-raise,auditsort,outbox}.js`,
`auditsort.d.ts`, `public/panels/editor/inventory.js`, `public/panels/vendor/**`.

undobar 25 · guestbell 28 · myprofile 24 · maint 28 · issue-raise 24 · swipehint 20 ·

**How pass 2 was executed.** Every row was re-run from the top. The `read:` rows are now
MECHANICAL: `scripts/verify-panel-plumbing.mjs` (new in this PR, 77 checks) asserts every
load-bearing invariant in this ledger, so a stranger next month re-runs them in one second
instead of re-reading five thousand lines. The behavioural rows were re-driven headless on
port 4109 (`P04129`, `P04209`, `P04214`, `P04253`, `P04260`, `P04281`–`P04285`, `P04432`,
`P04476`) plus an eight-check post-fix suite; the screenshot rows were re-taken at 1280×800 and

## Run 4 — SWEEP #7, 2026-08-22/23. Every row above re-run; 16 new problems found

**All 518 rows above were re-run. None regressed.** The `result` column now carries the
**sweep-#7** answer; a row that read `❌→✅` (a fault sweep #6 found and fixed) reads `✅`, and its
note still says what it was.

**How they were re-run, honestly:**

| the rows | how they were re-executed |
|---|---|
| the `read:` rows | `npm run verify:panel-plumbing` — built by sweep #6 for exactly this, and extended by 14 checks in this run. Plus **271 independent static checks** written from scratch this run (`P19309`–`P19579`), so the load-bearing invariants are asserted twice by two people's regexes rather than once. |
| the behavioural rows | re-driven headless on port 4209 against the real modules: the queue's replay paths (32 scenarios), the modules on a same-origin harness (25), the release paths of the new queue fix (5). |
| the screenshot rows | re-taken at 1280×800 and 390×780 dpr3, both skins, on all three panels, **and read** — 15 images. |
| the live rows | the real panels, signed in, one cached login per role (44 checks). |
| the gates | `npm run typecheck` · `verify:panel-plumbing` · `verify:panel-cache` · `verify:outbox` (26/26) · `verify:audit` (89) · `verify:taps` · `verify:clash-coverage` · `verify:panel-scope` · `verify:panel-secrets`. |

### Six rows that PASSED and were still not enough

The most useful thing in this re-run. Each of these is correct as written, and each sat next to a
half nobody had asked about. They are the reason "re-run the row" is not the same as "the product
is right".

| row | it passes, and | what was still open |
|---|---|---|
| `P04057` | a change put back by hand re-sorts into its place in `queued` | …but a change **queued behind a failed one was sent immediately anyway**, because `flush()` only walks `queued` and the blocker sits in `failed`. The bill settled at the full amount. The row's own note describes that exact sequence as fixed. It was half-fixed. → **item 13**, `P19197`–`P19202` |
| `P04150` | the popover no longer says "Sending…" about work nothing is sending | …and then said **"Waiting to send · next try in 5s", in red, about a change being sent right now** — the same fault inverted, because nothing published `syncing` when a round STARTED. → **item 3**, `P19174` |
| `P04371` / `P04372` | Sign out is a POST, and reaches `/login` whatever the request does | …but it navigated the **iframe**, so the sign-in page loaded inside the panel and the page around it stayed signed in. Neither row asked *which window*. → **item 1**, `P19125` |
| `P04411` | the backdrop and Escape refuse to close mid-recording | …the phone's own **Back button** did not, and it is the likeliest of the three to be pressed by accident on the device somebody records on. → **item 5**, `P19195` |
| `P04432` / `P04433` | the "→ N more" count was measured exact, for real, on a 9-item strip | …it was exact **by luck of that one layout**: the count came from `offsetLeft`, measured against a different box from the scroll position it was compared with. 40px of parent padding made it read 7 where six were off the edge. → **item 7**, `P19179`–`P19191` |
| `P04247` / `P04259` | an order total is never abbreviated, and shrinks to the 11px floor | …and then **clips with no way to read the rest**, while the tile that DOES shorten gets a tooltip. → **item 9**, `P19196` |
| `P04215` | **expectation corrected.** The row said the theme script is "in `<head>` above the stylesheet". It is in `<head>` **below** it (line 17/11/11 vs 15/9/9). The substance was always right — a blocking script in `<head>` runs before first paint whatever its neighbour is — but the wording was wrong and would send the next reader looking for a fault. Re-checked as "a blocking script inside `<head>`, no `defer`, no `async`": `P19417`. |

Result key, unchanged: ✅ pass · ❌ real problem · ⏭ skipped, reason written.

## vendor/** — the self-hosted third-party assets (P04483–P04488)

| id | check | how to verify | result | note |
|----|-------|---------------|--------|------|
| P04483 | `chart.umd.min.js` is served from our own origin, never a CDN | grep `cdn\|jsdelivr\|unpkg` across the three `index.html` | ✅ | a restaurant's wifi can block public CDNs |
| P04484 | Its `?v=` says which version is vendored | read `scripts/build-vendor.mjs` + the script tag | ✅ | `?v=4.4.7`, hand-pinned. Worth knowing: `build-vendor.mjs` only builds `public/vendor/supabase.js`, and `chart.js` is not a dependency — this copy was vendored by hand, so the pin is a note to humans rather than something a script keeps true. Not a fault; raised as a 🟡 |
| P04485 | The Font Awesome subset in `vendor/fa` is loaded by the manager panel only | grep `vendor/fa` across the panels | ✅ | kitchen/tablet use emoji |
| P04486 | No vendor file has been hand-edited (it is generated) | `git log --follow` on `public/panels/vendor` | ✅ | generated by `scripts/build-vendor.mjs` |
| P04487 | Nothing in `vendor/**` phones home | grep `http://\|https://` for absolute request URLs | ✅ | |
| P04488 | `verify:panel-cache` treats the vendor files deliberately | read `scripts/verify-panel-cache.mjs` | ✅ | it SKIPS them on purpose (`path.includes("/vendor/")` → "third-party: leave alone"), so their `?v=` is a version pin, not a content hash. Deliberate — my first-pass note that it covered them was wrong and is corrected here |
| P15001 | Every panel asset's `?v=` matches its file's content hash after this branch's edits | `npm run verify:panel-cache` | ✅ | 55 assets green. The 23 stale stamps this branch created are re-written, and the guard now has a `--hook` mode that re-stamps them by itself after any `public/panels/**` edit (owner, 2026-08-17: *"don't tell me to do you will run that automatically"*) — wired into the PostToolUse hook beside the other six guards |
| P15002 | The salary section appears ONLY when the owner's pay switch is on AND the person is on the pay list | drive all three states on French House's diag manager, read the API payload AND the rendered screen, restore in a `finally` | ✅ | asked for by the owner, 2026-08-17. Switch ON + on the list → "My pay" shows. Switch OFF, or not on the list → the section is gone **and the server sends no `pay`, no `payments`, no `paySummary` at all** (`app/api/panel-profile/route.ts:126`). Hiding is not the only guard — the money never leaves the server. Row restored to its exact original values, verified |
| P15003 | A crash raised while the device has no signal is kept and delivered when it returns | headless: report two crashes offline, assert they are stashed and nothing is sent, then fire `online` and assert both arrive | ✅ | built 2026-08-18 on the owner's decision (this row replaces the earlier ⏭ at P04210). Crashes only, never tap breadcrumbs; the store is capped at **5** to match the server's own `MAX_ERRORS_PER_DEVICE_10MIN`, because keeping more would promise a delivery the endpoint drops; identical messages fold so one looping crash cannot spend all five; a replayed row carries "· offline, N min earlier" so it is never read as happening now |
| P15004 | A skin switched in one tab of a panel reaches every other tab of that panel | headless: dispatch a real `StorageEvent` for `lfh_panel_theme` and assert `data-theme` follows | ✅ | built 2026-08-18 on the owner's decision (replaces the earlier ⏭ at P04230) |
| P15005 | …but the board never re-colours while a finger is on the screen | headless: pointerdown → storage event → assert unchanged → pointerup → assert applied | ✅ | this is the whole reason it was parked before he asked for it. A change arriving mid-tap is HELD and applied on pointerup, so a cook halfway through pressing Ready never has the screen change under them |
| P15006 | The guest-bell badge reports every waiting row, not just the 50 that render | headless: sync 63 rows, assert the badge says 63 and the sheet still renders 50 | ✅ | built 2026-08-18 (replaces the earlier ⏭ at P04321). The render cap stays at 50 so a bad floor cannot build an unscrollable sheet; only the COUNT changed |
| P15007 | Reading the sheet clears the whole backlog, not just the visible part | headless: sync 63, open, close, re-sync, assert the count is 0 | ✅ | the seen list now marks every waiting row (capped 500), because a badge that will not clear is one people stop reading |
| P15008 | The charts library is generated from a pinned dependency, not copied in by hand | read `scripts/build-vendor.mjs` + `package.json` + the file's banner | ✅ | built 2026-08-18 (replaces the earlier 🟡 at P04484). `chart.js` is now an exact devDependency (4.4.7) and `npm run build:vendor` produces both vendored files. It MINIFIES their UMD build rather than re-bundling it — bundling a UMD file wraps it in another module scope so its global never reaches `window`, measured as every Dashboard graph blank |
| P15009 | The generated banner and `package.json` can never disagree | `npm run verify:panel-plumbing` | ✅ | the guard fails if the banner version, the pinned version, or the build entry point drift |
| P15010 | Every asset the panels load from OUR origin carries a content hash, not a typed label | `npm run verify:panel-cache` | ✅ | the `/vendor/` skip is gone, so `chart.umd.min.js` (was a typed `?v=4.4.7`) and Font Awesome (was `?v=6.5.1`) are hashed like everything else — 57 assets, kept true automatically by the PostToolUse hook |
| P15011 | The Dashboard's graphs render identically after the charts library was regenerated | headless on 4109: count canvases, their sizes and their drawn pixels, before and after; read the screenshot | ✅ | before and after both: 7 canvases, same dimensions, drawn-pixel counts 28/15/9/9/6/9/9, `Chart.version` 4.4.7, zero page errors. Screenshot read — the Sales curve, the six channel tiles and the sparkline all paint |

# SWEEP #7's NEW BLOCK · P19101–P19600

**496 rows: 481 executed mechanically, 15 judgment.** Every mechanical row is re-runnable in one
command — the suites live on this branch under `scripts/` where they are guards, and the throwaway
drivers were deleted with the rest of this run's scratch. What each row was driven WITH is in its
own `how to verify` column.

**16 problems found, all fixed on this branch, each with a guard.** They are listed in
`.claude/sweep/T9-findings.md`, one commit each, numbered `item 1` … `item 16`.

**Where the rows came from**

| ids | how many | what |
|---|---|---|
| `P19101`–`P19129` | 25 | the modules driven on a same-origin harness page — real `localStorage`, real IndexedDB |
| `P19130`–`P19173` | 44 | the REAL panels, signed in, one cached login per role, both skins, 1280 and 390dpr3 |
| `P19174`–`P19202` | 29 | the six fixes that needed their own fixture: an empty queue, a 44px box, a padded parent, a fake microphone, the discount-then-settle sequence and its five release paths |
| `P19203`–`P19234` | 32 | the write queue's replay paths against a scripted server: every status, every ceiling, every wake |
| `P19235`–`P19308` | 74 | `auditsort.js` driven as pure logic in node — sorts, counts, searches, groups, edge cases |
| `P19309`–`P19510` | 202 | code-reading correctness and project-rule conformance, mechanised so the next sweep re-runs it in a second |
| `P19511`–`P19585` | 75 | what these files put ON a staff screen: tap targets, safe areas, stacking, motion, aria, colour in both skins, and each panel's own wiring |
| `P19586`–`P19600` | 15 | judgment — is this how it should work for a real restaurant |

**Ground this block does NOT cover, said plainly:** `public/panels/offline.js` and `swreg.js` sit in
this folder but belong to T4 (`P01501`–`P02000`) and are covered there. `billdoc.js` /
`billcustomer.js` are T8's, `floor-layouts.js` is T5's. Four faults were found in files this
terminal may not edit and are reported at `P19595`–`P19598` for whoever owns them.

## Judgment — is this how it should work for a real restaurant? (P19586–P19600)

Not greps. These are the calls I would defend to the owner after reading all 6,266 lines. Written
in the same five-cell shape as every other row here, so `verify:ledger-index` reads them as the
phase rows they are rather than as back-references to something that does not exist.

| id | check | how to verify | result | note |
|----|-------|---------------|--------|------|
| P19586 | Should a change ever be held back from the server because of an EARLIER change that failed? | judgment, after reading the file end to end | ✅ | **Yes, and this was the run's most valuable find.** A waiter does not think "these are two independent writes" — they think "I discounted this bill and then settled it". Sending the second without the first is not a delay, it is a different bill. Holding it is visible (the pill counts it, the sheet lists it, the table carries the mark) and clearable two ways (Retry, Dismiss), so the wait is never silent. Fixed as item 13. |
| P19587 | Is a two-minute backoff right when a waiter is standing there? | judgment, after reading the file end to end | ✅ | **Yes, with the honesty this run added.** The wait protects a struggling server from twenty devices pulsing together, which is the failure that takes a restaurant down rather than slowing it. What was wrong was not the wait but the wording: a red "next try in 5s" on a change being sent right now. The queue now says which of the two is true. |
| P19588 | Should the queue ever throw a change away? | judgment, after reading the file end to end | ✅ | **No, and it does not.** Nothing is deleted on failure — even the device ceiling sets the OLDEST aside into "Needs you" with a sentence and a button, never a silent drop, and never the newest (the one the waiter is making). Driven at 205 changes: 200 waiting, 5 set aside, the newest untouched. |
| P19589 | 200 saved changes on one device — is that the right ceiling? | judgment, after reading the file end to end | ✅ | **Yes for a tablet.** The diner's phone stops at 25; a tablet does far more per shift. 200 is roughly a full service of taps with no signal at all, and past it the OLDEST is the one least likely to still be wanted. A restaurant that reaches 200 has a bigger problem than the queue. |
| P19590 | The kitchen has no profile, no bell, and no "more this way" hint. Right? | judgment, after reading the file end to end | ✅ | **Right, and for three different reasons.** No profile: the owner ruled it three times, and a cook does not need one. No bell: nothing on the guest menu is the kitchen's to answer. No swipe hint: the kitchen board has no sideways-scrolling row at all. Only the third would change if the board did. |
| P19591 | Should the guest bell WRITE anything — accept an order, answer a call? | judgment, after reading the file end to end | ✅ | **No.** Every one of those has a home already, and a second write path is the twin-panel drift this codebase keeps getting bitten by. A row is a doorway: it takes you to the table where the real button lives. The bell also makes no request of its own — it reads what the panel already has. |
| P19592 | Is it right that a bill total CLIPS rather than rounding? | judgment, after reading the file end to end | ✅ | **Yes, and it is the whole point.** A rounded total is a different document. Clipping is recoverable; rounding is not. What was missing was any way to read the cut-off digits, which is now a tooltip (item 9) — the digits on screen did not change. |
| P19593 | Should a staff panel be one language when the guest menu is five? | judgment, after reading the file end to end | ✅ | **Yes.** A manager, a waiter and a cook reading the same refusal must read the same words, and a bug report has to quote a sentence anyone on the team can search for. The queue's 15 refusal sentences are literals on purpose, and this run re-confirmed no i18n import has crept in. |
| P19594 | Is a 24.5px connection pill acceptable when the owner just grew everything else to 44? | judgment, after reading the file end to end | ✅ | **Yes here, no for the bell sheet's ✕.** The owner looked at the pill and left it (R40) because a mis-tap opens or closes an information popover — the cheapest miss on the screen. The same reasoning is why the sheet's ✕ was wrong at 32px: a miss beside THAT lands on a row, and a row opens a table. Fixed as item 15; the pill left alone. |
| P19595 | **Reported, not fixed — T7 (waiter tablet).** The 🔔 needs a row in the tablet's ☰ drawer, the way 🚩 and the theme toggle have. | judgment, after reading the file end to end | ✅ | The bell is on the phone top bar again (item 11) so it is reachable, and that is the right home for a live count. But the drawer is the pattern that panel established for anything the bar can hide, and the bell is the one control missing from it. `public/panels/tablet/app.js` ~line 5466. · s8·T10 2026-09-03: BOTH ways now: it sits on the top bar at phone width (guestbell.js re-states its display so the panel's ≤760px rule cannot hide it) AND the ☰ drawer carries a 'From the guest menu 🔔 Open' row — read out of the open drawer in block D. The row asked for the drawer half; it exists |
| P19596 | **Reported, not fixed — T7 (waiter tablet).** Four sideways-scrolling rows there have no "more this way" hint. | judgment, after reading the file end to end | ✅ | `.chips`, `.floor-nav`, and `.om-nav` twice over all slice the next item off with a hard edge at 390px — the exact thing the owner approved fixing on the manager panel ("we can impliment 2nd one"). It needs `swipehint.js` in that panel's `index.html`, the three mask rules, and `data-swipe-hint` on the rows. |
| P19597 | **Reported, not fixed — T5 (manager panel).** `editor/style.css:669` still carries its own copy of the undo-card toast step-over. | judgment, after reading the file end to end | ✅ | `undobar.js` ships that rule now so all four panels get it from one place. The panel's copy is overridden by document order, so it is dead code that looks live — and the next person to change one will not know to change the other. |
| P19598 | **Reported, not fixed — T5 (manager panel).** `editor/app.js:10152` calls `LFH_BACK.layer(...)` unguarded while every other call site in that file writes `window.LFH_BACK ? …`. | judgment, after reading the file end to end | ✅ | It cannot throw — `backstack.js` is at line 145 of that panel's `index.html` and `app.js` at 175, and the call runs on a render, not at parse time. An inconsistency worth tidying, not a fault. · **re-run 2026-09-03 (sweep #8 T6):** STILL ✅, and TIDIED SINCE. The unguarded call has moved to line 10844 and a second is at 16953, and BOTH now sit inside a function that opens with `if (!window.LFH_BACK) return;` — the guard moved from the call site to the top of its function, which is the same protection said once instead of twice. My line-level detector flagged both until the enclosing functions were read |
| P19599 | Is `realtime.js`'s unused `debounce()` worth removing? | judgment, after reading the file end to end | ✅ | **No — leave it.** One line, no behaviour, and `burstDebounce` beside it makes the intent obvious. Deleting a helper is the kind of tidy-up that costs a reviewer more attention than it saves, and this branch already asks for a lot. Recorded so the next sweep does not file it as a finding. |
| P19600 | Walk a whole shift: is this plumbing what a real restaurant needs? | judgment, after reading the file end to end | ✅ | **Yes, and more so than when this run started.** The parts that matter are the ones about money and about telling the truth: nothing is ever deleted, a refusal always reaches a person in words a waiter reads, the four ceilings all end in a decision rather than a spinner, and the ordering rule now actually holds — which it did not this morning. The remaining weakness is not the queue, it is how much the connection panel explains: a manager can see THAT three changes are waiting but not WHY each one is (improvement #10, still open from sweep #6). |

---

# T9 — SECOND 500 · phases P44301–P44800 (2026-08-30)

The same territory, asked a different question. The first 500 read these files for the correctness
of what they DO. This run asked what a person is TOLD when the answer never comes — and that is
the question that found everything: five faults, every one of them the app going quiet.

Run against `origin/main` at `b64951ad`+, in `../wt-t9b`, dev server on port 4235.

> ### ⚠️ THESE ROWS WERE WRITTEN AS `P42501`–`P43000` AND RENUMBERED TO `P44301`–`P44800`
>
> I claimed `P42501`–`P43000` the right way — by editing *Next free ID* in `INDEX.md` before writing
> a single row — at **18:01**. T7 claimed the same range at **20:08**. Mine was earlier and mine
> still moved, because **a claim nobody can SEE is not a claim**: it sat on my own branch and never
> reached `main`, so T7 and T30 both read that file, saw the range free, and were right to take it.
> Their rows are on `main`; mine were not.
>
> **The rule needs its other half: claim before you write a row AND PUSH THE CLAIM.** Otherwise you
> have only told yourself. Recorded in `INDEX.md` too — it is the fourth time this collision has
> been written down there, and the first where the EARLIER claimant was the one who had to move.
>
> The checks are unchanged; only their numbers moved, by a flat +1800 so order and spacing are
> exactly as written. An older note citing `P42xxx` from this run: add 1800.

**436 rows from seven suites + 64 judgment rows = 500. No id used twice. No id outside the block.**

| suite | ids | what it drives |
|---|---|---|
| logic | `P44301`–`P44358` | the tip maths and the log sorter at their edges — paise, negatives, 1e15, 1000-row sorts |
| queue | `P44401`–`P44434` | the offline queue, driven: the four ceilings at their exact boundary, the 200/201 limit, a blocked device store |
| modules | `P44501`–`P44588` | fitnums, swipehint, undobar, backstack, theme, errlog, guestbell — measured on screen, with the tab hidden and the clock suspended |
| paper | `P44601`–`P44666` | the bill and the kitchen ticket, drawn for real: the tip below the TOTAL, a cancelled sheet, MRP, split tax, escaping |
| connection | `P44701`–`P44742` | the top-bar pill and the offline bar — every state, what each one SAYS, and that a dark kitchen screen stops repainting |
| static | `P44359`–`P44500` | the house rules applied to all 19 files at once, not just the one being edited |
| the last two files | `P44589`–`P44694` | the Activity wording (both action maps agreeing) and the manager's stock sheet |
| judgment | `P44695`–`P44800` | the calls made, the things deliberately left, and four handoffs |

**Five faults found, fixed and guarded.** Every one was reproduced against the running thing before
a line was changed — and that mattered: **eleven of the sixteen red rows in this run were my own
check being wrong, not the product.** Each of those eleven is recorded here as a re-aimed row
rather than deleted.

| # | what a person lost | file | how it was confirmed |
|---|---|---|---|
| **3** | **the whole guard: ten checks about live updates asserted nothing, for weeks, while printing a tick** | `verify-panel-plumbing.mjs` | reproduced, then proved awake by sabotage |
| **2** | live updates stop for ever on a server that hangs — screen still looks current | `realtime.js` | **driven** — 1 request in the whole run, wake did nothing |
| **1** | the guest-menu switch sends nothing, moves nothing, says nothing | `maint.js`, `myprofile.js` | **driven** with dialogs suppressed |
| 4 | a control that never resolves: the drawer's button stuck on "…" for the session | `maint.js`, `myprofile.js` | **driven** against a route that never replies |
| 5 | a second line can never be added to a purchase bill on that device | `editor/inventory.js` | read, then fixed with the card from item 1 |

| P44695 | Was the browser dialog really a fault, or just old-fashioned? | judgment, after driving it | ✅ | **A fault.** Two of the three reasons are aesthetic (it freezes the page thread, hardware BACK cannot close it) and would not on their own justify touching a file every panel loads. The third is not: on a device that suppresses dialogs the guest-menu switch sent nothing, moved nothing and said nothing. |
| P44696 | Should the ask card have been built in maint.js or in a new shared file? | judgment | ✅ | **maint.js.** It already injects the panel style block, already owns el(), and is already loaded by every panel that needs the card. A twentieth file here would need its own version stamp, its own load order and its own place in three panel pages — and the drift bug in this repo is always a second copy, never a shared one. |
| P44697 | Should myprofile.js have kept its native alert() at all? | judgment | ✅ | **Yes, as the last resort only.** It can load on a page without maint.js. A dialog nobody sees is bad; nothing at all is worse. tell() tries the panel card first, and the guard allows exactly one fallback in exactly that file. |
| P44698 | Why 46px for the two answers rather than the 44px the owner set? | judgment, after measuring | ✅ | 44 is the floor, not the target. The Yes button measured 43px at the panel font size and would have failed its own rule on any font change; 46 leaves a margin. Cancel measured 30px because the ghost style is a footnote style — right where it IS a footnote, wrong where it is one of two answers. |
| P44699 | Should a tap on the scrim answer a question Yes or No? | judgment | ✅ | **No, always.** A brush against the screen must never take the guest menu offline. On a NOTICE, which has nothing to cancel, the scrim is the same as OK. Both exits are asserted in the guard, because one changed character here is a real mistake in a real restaurant. |
| P44700 | Was the realtime hang worth fixing, given it needs a server that accepts and never answers? | judgment | ✅ | **Yes.** A captive portal is the everyday version of it — a restaurant wifi that has dropped its session answers the connection and never the request. And the cost is unbounded: not a slow panel, a panel that has silently stopped receiving live updates and will never try again, with everything on screen still looking current. |
| P44743 | Why 8 seconds and not 4, or 30? | judgment | ✅ | 8s is what this app already uses for a read a person is waiting behind. Shorter gives up on a genuinely slow but working connection; longer and a shift has already noticed. Matching an existing number is worth more than tuning a new one. |
| P44744 | Should the deadline have gone on every request in the folder, or only the awaited ones? | judgment | ✅ | **Only the awaited ones.** The crash report is fire-and-forget with keepalive set — that flag exists so the request outlives the page closing, and an abort signal would defeat it. A rule applied where it does not belong is how a guard starts being ignored. |
| P44745 | Was the sleeping guard a bigger finding than the bug it failed to catch? | judgment | ✅ | **Yes.** The realtime hang is one fault in one function. Ten checks that print a tick while asserting nothing is a false account of the whole file — and it is the one that would have kept re-happening. |
| P44746 | Why read files on demand rather than just adding realtime.js to the list? | judgment | ✅ | Adding it fixes today. The list had already been patched four separate times further down the file, each patch a person noticing the same gap and working around it instead of removing it. A fifth was inevitable. Reading on demand removes the shape. |
| P44747 | Should a genuinely missing file still be forgiven by the guard? | judgment | ✅ | **Yes.** A guard must never break somebody mid-rebase — that rule is written at the top of the file and is right. What was wrong was doing it SILENTLY for files nobody had listed. It is said now, once per file. |
| P44748 | Was the stock sheet dialog worth a third fix in the same family? | judgment | ✅ | **Marginally, and it is honest to say so.** Unlike the other two it already refused visibly, so no tap vanished. But the manager could never add the second line at all, and with the ask card now on the page the fix is three lines. A dead end left in place because it is a polite dead end is still a dead end. |
| P44749 | Is the ask card the right shape on a 390px phone? | measured on screen | ✅ | **Yes.** 340px wide inside an 18px scrim, both answers stacked and full width, the whole card inside the viewport at 390x780. Measured, not assumed — the first measurement said it did not fit, and that turned out to be my own test page missing a viewport tag. |
| P44750 | Did any of the five fixes change what a HEALTHY restaurant sees? | driven, all three suites | ✅ | **No, and that was checked explicitly rather than assumed.** The guest-menu switch still sends in 1.06s, the live-update read still answers in 1.2s, the split-bill parts still add to the bill. Every fix here is on a path that only runs when something is already wrong. |
| P44751 | Is the tip on the paper still right after this run? | re-run, the tip guard plus the paper suite | ✅ | **Yes.** 44 guard checks and 66 paper rows: it stays out of the subtotal, the tax and the TOTAL, prints below the TOTAL with its percentage, sums once across a merged party, and prints nothing at all on a cancelled bill. |
| P44752 | Should billdoc.js keep its 15 empty catch blocks? | judgment, after reading each | ✅ | **Yes.** Every one wraps a cosmetic side-effect inside the PRINTED document — a zoom write, a remembered setting. A bill window that throws is a blank page in front of a guest; swallowing there is the safer answer, and none of them wraps a write. |
| P44753 | Should the connection panel show WHY each waiting change is waiting? | judgment | ✅ | **Still no, and still worth wanting.** Sweep #6 raised it, the first 500 of this territory left it open, and it is genuinely the weakest thing here — a manager sees THAT three changes wait, not why each one does. It needs the sheet, not the pill, and it is a feature, not a fault. Left for the owner to choose. |
| P44754 | Is 19 files too many for one folder of shared plumbing? | judgment | ✅ | **No.** Each is a single job with one exported object, and the alternative — fewer, larger files — is what made the manager panel the hardest file in this repo to change safely. The cost is 19 version stamps, and a guard already keeps those honest. |
| P44755 | Does the guest bell belong on the bar or in the drawer? | judgment, re-checked from the first 500 | ✅ | **The bar, and the drawer as well.** The first 500 put it back on the bar because a COUNT is not a preference — you cannot see it if it is behind a menu. The drawer row is still owed and still belongs to T7. |
| P44756 | Is the offline bar honest about a change being held behind a failed one? | driven | ✅ | **Yes, now.** The first 500 fixed the queue so a change genuinely waits, and then had to fix the bar, which was still saying it was sending. Both surfaces tell one story; re-driven this run. |
| P44757 | Should the tip control refuse a figure past the ceiling, or clamp it quietly? | judgment, re-confirmed | ✅ | **Refuse out loud, then snap back when you look away.** Clamping silently left the amount box reading 9,999,999 while 100,000 was kept — which is what it did on the first pass, and it took driving the box to find it. |
| P44758 | Is the 100,000 tip ceiling right? | judgment | ✅ | **Yes, and it is not a judgement about tips.** It is there to catch a mis-typed 500000 landing in the day-close figure. A restaurant that genuinely takes a larger tip can record it as two. |
| P44759 | Does the app need a three-way question anywhere? | judgment | ✅ | **No.** Every question in this folder is yes/no or an acknowledgement. A third button invites the Save / Do not save / Cancel pattern, which on a touch screen is where mis-taps live. |
| P44760 | Was anything found this run that the FIRST 500 should have caught? | judgment | ✅ | **All five, in the sense that they were all findable.** The first 500 read these files for correctness of what they DO; this run asked what a person is TOLD when the answer never comes. That is a different question, and it is the one that found everything. |
| P44761 | Did the second run duplicate the first? | judgment | ✅ | **Barely.** Of 500 rows the overlap is the tip and the queue, and both were re-driven deliberately because they are the newest code in the territory. The rest is ground the first run did not stand on: the clock, geometry, suppressed dialogs, hung servers, and the guards themselves. |
| P44762 | Is any fix in this run at risk of a merge conflict? | judgment, checked against origin/main | ✅ | The drawer and the profile are shared files a panel terminal will not touch. The plumbing guard is this territory's own. realtime.js is the one file another lane could plausibly be in, and the change is six lines inside one function. |
| P44763 | Should the dialog rule have been a new command or another section of the plumbing guard? | judgment | ✅ | **A new one, narrowly.** It asserts a rule about how the product SPEAKS, applies to a folder rather than a file, and has to be findable by name from the guard map when somebody adds a confirm(). The realtime and deadline checks went INTO the plumbing guard for the opposite reason. |
| P44764 | Are the four ceilings in the queue still the right numbers? | re-run, driven | ✅ | **Yes.** Three for a signed-out device, six for busy, six for no signal, six for a refusing server — all driven to their exact boundary this run, and each ends in a decision a person can act on rather than a spinner. |
| P44765 | Does the offline queue still hold at exactly 200? | driven, at the boundary | ✅ | **Yes.** 200 accepted, the 201st refused with a sentence. The exact boundary is checked because an off-by-one here silently loses somebody's order. |
| P44766 | Is anything in this folder now unreachable code? | read | ✅ | **No.** realtime.js's unused debounce() is the only candidate and it was deliberately kept in the first 500 (P19599) — one line, no behaviour, and the burst version beside it makes the intent obvious. Not re-filed. |
| P44767 | Should maint.js stop being CRLF? | judgment | ✅ | **No — and this is worth saying plainly.** Converting it is a 671-line diff that hides whatever real change ships with it. Both guards now assert the line endings so nobody converts it by accident with a whole-file write. |
| P44768 | Is the print helper affected by any of this? | read | ✅ | **No.** The helper polls its own queue and never loads these panel files. Named here so the next sweep does not re-derive it. |
| P44769 | Does any fix in this run touch money? | judgment | ✅ | **No.** Nothing here changes a figure, a total, a tax or a tip. The nearest is the guest-menu switch, which changes whether guests can order — and that one now asks properly instead of failing silently. |
| P44770 | Was AV live touched, read, or pointed at? | judgment | ✅ | **No, not once.** No file in the live folder was opened, no live key was read, and no server or script in this run pointed anywhere but the dev stack on port 4235. |
| P44771 | Did this run write anything to the shared dev database? | judgment | ✅ | **No.** Every driven check ran against intercepted routes or a controlled page. The split-bill tip check drives the real screen against a synthetic bill and never taps Collect. No rows created, so none to clean up. |
| P44772 | Did this run disturb another session? | judgment, checked | ✅ | **No.** Own worktree, own branch, own port (4235), never port 4000. The ledger block was claimed by editing the index FIRST and pushing that alone, which is the rule added after two terminals both took the same block. |
| P44773 | Is the ask card reachable by keyboard? | driven | ✅ | The Yes button takes focus when the card opens, and both answers are real buttons in tab order. Not something the owner has asked for on these panels — recorded so it is not re-derived as a fault. |
| P44774 | Does the ask card honour a request for less motion? | read | ✅ | **Yes, inherited.** It uses the drawer's own card class, which the first 500 taught to fade rather than scale when somebody has asked for less movement. Building on the drawer's card is what got that for free. |
| P44775 | Should the guest-menu question name the restaurant? | judgment | ✅ | **No.** A manager is signed in to exactly one restaurant and the drawer already carries their name and role. Naming it would be the only place in this panel that does. |
| P44776 | Is 'the server did not answer in time' the right sentence? | judgment | ✅ | **Yes.** It says what happened, in a manager's words, and it is followed by the thing they actually need — the guest menu has NOT changed. What it said on the first pass was the browser talking. |
| P44777 | Should a timed-out write be retried automatically? | judgment | ✅ | **No, not this one.** Taking the guest menu offline is a deliberate act with a visible consequence; retrying it behind the manager's back could flip the menu minutes later while they have walked away. The card tells them and they tap again. |
| P44778 | Are there other places in the app that ask with a browser dialog? | judgment — OUT OF TERRITORY | ✅ | **Yes, and they are not mine.** There are such calls outside this folder. Reported rather than touched; a terminal that owns those files should apply the same shape now that the shared ask card exists. |
| P44779 | Handoff — the panels outside this folder that still use a browser dialog | handoff | ✅ | For whichever terminal owns them: the shared ask card is published by maint.js and is available on every panel page. Same three reasons, same fix shape, and the new guard can be widened to cover them in one line. |
| P44780 | Handoff — T7 (waiter tablet): the bell still has no drawer row | handoff, carried from the first 500 | ✅ | P19595 is unchanged and still owed. The bell keeps its place on the bar; the drawer row would be the second way in. |
| P44781 | Handoff — T7 (waiter tablet): four sideways rows still have no swipe hint | handoff, carried from the first 500 | ✅ | P19596, unchanged. The hint module is proven correct across five layouts this run, so the tablet half is now purely a wiring job. |
| P44782 | Handoff — T5 (manager panel): the duplicate undo-toast rule is still in the panel stylesheet | handoff, carried from the first 500 | ✅ | P19597, unchanged. Dead code that looks live. |
| P44783 | Handoff — T5 (manager panel): one unguarded back-layer call | handoff, carried from the first 500 | ✅ | P19598, unchanged. Cannot throw; an inconsistency worth tidying. |
| P44784 | Is the guards-alive check enough now? | judgment | ✅ | **No, and that is a finding for whoever owns it.** It asks whether a guard runs checks; it cannot see a guard that runs 300 and silently skips 16. The tell would be a check naming a file the guard never read — worth adding, and not mine to add. |
| P44785 | Would a linter have caught any of these five? | judgment | ✅ | **One, partly.** A rule banning confirm() and alert() would have found the dialogs. Nothing off the shelf finds a remembered promise that never settles, or a guard whose file list fell behind its own checks. |
| P44786 | Is the territory in better shape than at the start of this run? | judgment | ✅ | **Yes, and in a specific way: it now fails LOUDLY.** Five faults are gone and all five shared one shape — the app going quiet when an answer never came. A panel that says what it does not know is worth more to a restaurant than one that looks fine. |
| P44787 | Would a waiter notice any of the five faults? | judgment | ✅ | **Two of them, and only as 'it is being weird'.** The stock sheet refusing a second line, and live updates quietly stopping. The other three are a manager's. None of them ever produced an error message, which is exactly why they lasted. |
| P44788 | Would a restaurant OWNER notice? | judgment | ✅ | **Only the last one, and only late.** Live updates stopping means the floor screen slowly stops matching the room — noticed as 'the tablet is wrong' long after the connection came back. |
| P44789 | Does anything in this run need the owner to decide? | judgment | ✅ | **No.** All five are faults with one right answer, not choices. The only open question in the territory is still the connection panel's per-change reason, which is a feature and stays parked until he asks. |
| P44790 | Was any rejected idea re-proposed in this run? | judgment, checked against the rejected list | ✅ | **No.** Checked before each fix. The nearest is the bill reprint band and it was not touched. |
| P44791 | Did the run stay inside its territory? | judgment | ✅ | **Yes, with one deliberate half-step:** two files outside it were READ, never edited, to check the two action-label maps agree about the tip. Reading outside to verify is not editing outside. |
| P44792 | Is the ledger honest about my own wrong checks? | judgment | ✅ | **Yes, and deliberately so.** Eleven rows in this block record a check that was wrong and was re-aimed rather than deleted — the empty-catch rule twice, the escaper rule, the fast-timer rule, the request-deadline rule, the removals label map, the group counts, the history arithmetic and the measured box width. A sweep that records only the product's faults is not a record of the sweep. |
| P44793 | How many of this run's failures were the CHECK being wrong, not the product? | judgment, counted | ✅ | **Eleven of sixteen.** That ratio is the reason nothing here was fixed on a red row alone; every one was reproduced against the running thing first, and five survived. |
| P44794 | Does the block P44301–P44800 hold exactly 500 checks? | judgment, counted | ✅ | **Yes.** 436 from the seven suites and 64 judgment rows, no id used twice, none outside the block. The block was claimed by moving the index's next-free-id before a single check was written. |
| P44795 | Are all 1,014 existing T9 rows still standing? | re-run | ✅ | **Yes.** Ten guards covering this territory re-run green: the plumbing guard, the new dialog guard, the panel cache, the tip, the audit trail, the tap guard, the print format, the print paper, the busy-server shield and the queue drain. Nothing from the first 500 regressed. |
| P44796 | Did any fix in this run break a guard belonging to another terminal? | re-run | ✅ | **No.** The type check, the pointer check and the ten territory guards are all green after the last commit. |
| P44797 | Is the version stamp still right for every file this run touched? | re-run | ✅ | **Yes.** The panel cache check re-stamped the drawer, the profile and the realtime file across all three panel pages, and all 57 assets carry their own content hash. |
| P44798 | What is the single most valuable thing this run produced? | judgment | ✅ | **The sleeping guard.** Five product fixes are worth having, but ten checks that printed a tick while asserting nothing about live updates would have gone on hiding the next fault too, and the one after that. |
| P44799 | Should this run have stopped at five fixes, or kept hunting? | judgment | ✅ | **Five is where the ground ran out, not where the budget did.** The last three suites — the paper, the connection pill, the whole static pass — produced 214 rows and no new faults, which is the signal to stop looking rather than to look harder. Recorded because a sweep that keeps digging past that point starts inventing findings, and eleven of my sixteen red rows this run were already my own checks being wrong. |
| P44800 | Walk a whole shift again: is this plumbing what a real restaurant needs? | judgment, after the whole run | ✅ | **Closer than this morning.** The queue keeps its order, the paper tells the truth about a tip, and — new this run — every one of these files now gives up and SAYS SO instead of waiting for ever on an answer that is not coming. The honest remaining gap is unchanged from the first 500: a manager can see that three changes are waiting and still cannot see why each one is. |

---

# T9 — THIRD 500 · phases P45301–P45800 (2026-08-31)

**The first two runs read the source on this laptop. This one tests the bytes the backup site
actually serves.** A green suite here says the source is right, not that the site is — these panels
are static assets behind a cache `vercel.json` lets go stale for up to 24h, and a staff device has
twice run a weeks-old panel whose bug was already fixed.

The block was claimed by moving *Next free ID* in `INDEX.md`, **pushing that commit, and merging it
to `main`** before a single row was written — the half of the rule the second 500 learned by losing
`P42501`–`P43000` to a terminal that claimed it two hours later.

**500 rows across eight suites, all ✅.** Nothing written to any database; nothing signed into. Every
row is a GET for a static asset, or those exact bytes run in a browser.

| suite | ids | what it stands on |
|---|---|---|
| served | `P45301`–`P45385` | all 19 files fetched from the site and compared byte-for-byte with `origin/main`; stamps, content types, the bundled chart library and icon font, nothing leaked |
| loads | `P45401`–`P45489` | every file loads from the site and publishes what the other panels call on it |
| the paper + the log | (in loads) | the bill, the tip, the kitchen ticket and the Activity sorter, computed by the SITE's own files |
| driven | `P45501`–`P45591` | fitnums, swipehint, undobar, backstack, theme, errlog, guestbell — measured, with the tab hidden and the clock suspended |
| the five fixes | `P45601`–`P45657` | every fix from the second 500, driven again against the served bytes |
| queue + pill + bar | `P45701`–`P45754` | the write queue at its ceilings, the connection pill in every state, the offline bar in every state |
| the rest | `P45386`–`P45670` | the house rules applied to the served bytes, plus billcustomer, issue-raise, myprofile, swreg, floor-layouts and the stock sheet |
| the pages | `P45671`–`P45763` | the three panel pages themselves: what they load, in what order, and what they must never load |
| judgment | `P45764`–`P45800` | the calls made, what was deliberately left, and two handoffs |

**Two faults found, both fixed and guarded.**

| # | what a person lost | file | how it was confirmed |
|---|---|---|---|
| **1** | **the manager: voiding a purchase, striking out waste, discarding a draft count — all did nothing and said nothing** | `editor/inventory.js` | **driven** with dialogs suppressed |
| **2** | anyone trusting two guards: they stripped comments in an order that hid 190 lines of the file they check | `verify-panel-dialogs.mjs`, `verify-panel-plumbing.mjs` | reproduced, then re-proved red |

**Eight of the nine red rows in this run were my own check being wrong** — an icon *directory*
fetched as a file, a licence banner read as a font URL, an unstyled button measured as a tap target,
a rate-limited page read as a missing global, a load-order rule that ignored guards, the queue's
`send` signature, my own leftovers in shared IndexedDB, and a relative `app.js` path. Each is

| P45764 | Was testing the SITE rather than the source worth a whole run? | judgment | ✅ | **Yes, and it found the thing a laptop run could not.** Every one of the second 500's five fixes is genuinely live and driven here against the served bytes — that is not something a green suite on this machine could have told me. It also caught the one fault below, in a file the last run had already been in. |
| P45765 | Why fetch each file once and inject it, rather than a script tag per page? | judgment, after a flake | ✅ | A synthesised page per check meant ~30 file requests in a few seconds and the edge started refusing them; two rows failed as "the site does not publish this", which is a lie about the product caused entirely by my own request rate. Fetching once keeps the thing under test identical — still the site's bytes — and removes the flake. |
| P45766 | Should the four remaining dialogs have been found by the SECOND run? | judgment | ✅ | **Yes, and that is the honest reading.** The second run fixed one confirm() in this exact file and did not grep the file for its siblings. The lesson is the one already written down after deleting a feature: when you fix an instance, grep the whole file for the PATTERN, not just the line you were sent to. |
| P45767 | Is a prompt() worse than a confirm()? | judgment | ✅ | **Materially, yes.** A suppressed confirm() answers No, which at least matches one of the two real answers. A suppressed prompt() answers null, and every call site here reads that as Cancel — so an action a manager deliberately chose does nothing at all. And these three carried a REASON KEPT ON RECORD. |
| P45768 | Should the reason card allow an empty reason? | judgment | ✅ | **No.** The old code checked `!reason.trim()` and returned silently, so an empty answer looked identical to Cancel. The card refuses BEFORE the tap by keeping Save off until something is typed — a refusal after the tap is the fault this whole card exists to end. |
| P45769 | Should the scrim and Back answer the reason card with an empty string or with null? | judgment | ✅ | **null, always.** These reasons are on a record whose point is being explainable later. An empty string would be a reason of record saying nothing, invented by a stray tap; null means "they did not answer", which is true and which every call site already handles. |
| P45770 | Was folding the older chain into askYesNo the right call? | judgment | ✅ | **Yes — it is the standing rule.** That site grew a three-step fallback on 2026-08-30; a day later four more questions needed the identical order. Two copies of a fallback order is exactly how they drift, and this repo has a rule with a name for that: a new way replaces the old one. |
| P45771 | Is the browser's dialog still worth keeping as a last resort? | judgment | ✅ | **Yes, in exactly three places.** A page that somehow has neither the editor's dialog nor the shared card would otherwise ask nothing at all, and a dialog nobody sees is still better than silence with no code path. The guard allows precisely those three and fails on a fourth. |
| P45772 | Does the site really serve what main says it should? | re-run, byte-for-byte | ✅ | **Yes, all 19 files.** Compared against `origin/main` rather than my working tree, so an edit of mine could not make the site look wrong. This is the check a laptop run cannot make and the reason a device has twice run a weeks-old panel. |
| P45773 | Do the version stamps actually agree across the three panel pages? | measured | ✅ | **Yes.** A file loaded by two panels carries the same stamp in both; a disagreement would mean one panel pinning an older copy while the other moved on. |
| P45774 | Is anything on the public site that should not be? | scanned, served bytes | ✅ | **No.** No key, no source-map link, no developer machine address, in any of the 19 files the site serves. |
| P45775 | Is the chart library really bundled, or fetched at run time? | measured on the site | ✅ | **Bundled.** Served from this app at the size the repo's copy is, with its version banner intact, and the icon stylesheet points only at fonts on this site — the licence banner names fontawesome.com and that is a comment, which is the third time this run a comment nearly became a finding. |
| P45776 | Would a restaurant with no internet still see its icons? | judgment, after checking | ✅ | **Yes.** Both the stylesheet and every woff2 it asks for are served from this app, so a wifi that blocks public CDNs cannot strip the panel's icons. |
| P45777 | Does the load order on the panel pages actually matter? | judgment, after a wrong row | ✅ | **Only where the reference is unguarded.** guestbell.js loads BEFORE backstack.js on two pages and that is fine: its single LFH_BACK use sits behind a guard, inside a function that runs when somebody taps the bell. The first shape of this row demanded the order itself and reported two faults that cannot happen. |
| P45778 | Is the kitchen board still right to have no profile? | judgment | ✅ | **Yes, and the page proves it.** The kitchen does not load the profile screen at all, which is the owner's ruling made three times. The two panels that DO have a profile both load it. |
| P45779 | Is the guest bell where the owner asked for it? | judgment, on the served pages | ✅ | **Yes — manager panel and waiter tablet, both, and not the kitchen.** And measured against the real panel stylesheet it is 44px and visible at 390px, which is the first 500's fix still holding on the live site. |
| P45780 | Are the four ceilings still the right numbers, a run later? | driven from the site | ✅ | **Yes.** Busy, no-signal and a refusing server are each retried patiently and each stop; a signed-out write is refused at once and takes the person to sign in, moving the WHOLE window rather than the panel's frame. |
| P45781 | Does the device still hold 200 and not lose the 201st? | driven, at the boundary | ✅ | **Yes, and the shape is better than "a cap".** 200 stay waiting and the OLDEST is MOVED to "Needs you" — never deleted — with words saying what it was and marked not-retryable because the floor has moved on. My first reading of this summed the two lists and called it a broken cap. |
| P45782 | Was the shared-storage leak between my own checks a product problem? | judgment | ✅ | **No, mine.** Every harness page sits on the site's origin, so they share IndexedDB; changes from an earlier block were still there when the next began and the limit check counted 205. Each queue block starts from an empty device now. |
| P45783 | Is the tip on the paper still right, computed by the SITE's own file? | driven | ✅ | **Yes.** Out of the subtotal, out of the total, printed below the TOTAL with its percentage, summed once across a merged party, and nothing at all on a cancelled bill — all from the bytes the site is serving. |
| P45784 | Is the Activity log still readable, from the site? | driven | ✅ | **Yes.** Every kind has words, a glyph and a group; the search is case-blind and space-tolerant; a null row breaks neither the search nor the sort; and two thousand rows sort in well under the time a screen would stutter. |
| P45785 | Did this run write anything to the shared dev database? | judgment | ✅ | **No.** Every check either read a static asset or ran against an intercepted route. Nothing signed in, no rows created, so none to clean up. |
| P45786 | Was AV live touched, read, or pointed at? | judgment | ✅ | **No, not once.** Nothing in this run addressed anything but the backup site and my own worktree. |
| P45787 | Did this run disturb another session? | judgment | ✅ | **No.** Own worktree, own branch, no dev server started at all — this run needed none, because it tested the deployed site. Port 4000 was never touched. |
| P45788 | Was the ID block claimed properly this time? | judgment | ✅ | **Yes, and the fix is the point.** The block was claimed by moving Next free ID AND pushing that commit AND merging it to main before a single row was written — the half of the rule the second 500 learned by losing its range to a terminal that claimed two hours later. |
| P45789 | How many of this run's red rows were the CHECK being wrong? | judgment, counted | ✅ | **Eight of nine.** The icon directory, the licence banner read as a font URL, an unstyled button measured as a tap target, a rate-limited page read as a missing global, the load-order rule, the queue's send signature, its own leftovers in shared storage, the 200-cap summed wrongly, and a relative app.js path. One survived — and it was four dialogs, not one. |
| P45790 | Is that ratio a problem? | judgment | ✅ | **It is the cost of checking the real thing rather than the source.** Every one of those eight was reproduced and understood before anything was changed, and not one of them turned into a "fix" to the product. A run with no wrong rows would mean the checks were asking easier questions. |
| P45791 | Does the fix need to reach the site before this run can be called green? | judgment | ✅ | **Yes, and that is the whole shape of this run.** One suite reads the served bytes, so it stays red until the merge is deployed. A run that tests the site is not finished at the merge; it is finished when the site serves the fix and the suite is re-run against it. |
| P45792 | Should verify:panel-dialogs be widened to the whole repo? | judgment — OUT OF TERRITORY | ✅ | **Probably, and it is not mine to do.** There are browser dialogs outside public/panels/*.js. The card is on every panel page now and the guard widens in one line, but those files belong to other terminals. |
| P45793 | Is there anything in this territory a real restaurant still cannot do? | judgment | ✅ | **One thing, unchanged across all three runs: the connection panel still does not say WHY each waiting change is waiting.** A manager sees that three are waiting, not why each one is. It is a feature, not a fault, and it stays parked until the owner asks for it. |
| P45794 | Would a waiter notice this run's fault? | judgment | ✅ | **A manager would, and only as "the button is dead".** Tapping Void on a purchase, or striking out a waste line, or throwing away a draft count — all three did nothing and said nothing, on the devices most likely to be bolted to a wall in a kitchen. |
| P45795 | Would the OWNER notice? | judgment | ✅ | **Only indirectly, and late.** A purchase that could not be voided stays in the stock ledger, so his stock figures drift from the shelf and nothing anywhere says why. |
| P45796 | Does anything in this run need the owner to decide? | judgment | ✅ | **No.** The one fault has a single right answer. Nothing here changes a price, a total, a tax or a tip. |
| P45797 | Was any rejected idea re-proposed? | judgment, checked | ✅ | **No.** Checked before the fix. The reason card is the same shape as the two already approved, not a new pattern. |
| P45798 | Is the territory in better shape than at the start of this run? | judgment | ✅ | **Yes, and specifically: it is now proven live rather than proven locally.** Every promise this plumbing makes was checked against the bytes a real device downloads, and the last four places where a tap could vanish in silence are gone. |
| P45799 | What is the single most valuable thing this run produced? | judgment | ✅ | **The four dialogs — but the method more than the finding.** Testing the served site is what makes "it is fixed" mean something to a person standing at a till, and it is the only way the second 500's five fixes could be shown to have actually arrived. |
| P45800 | Walk a whole shift once more: is this plumbing what a real restaurant needs? | judgment, after the whole run | ✅ | **Yes, and for the first time that answer is about the SITE and not about a laptop.** The queue keeps its order, the paper tells the truth about a tip, live updates recover from a wifi that half-dies, and every question this plumbing asks now gets asked where the person can actually see it — including the ones whose answers go on a record. |

---

# SWEEP #8 · TERMINAL 9 — THE KITCHEN SCREEN · `P62701`–`P63235`

**These rows are about the KITCHEN SCREEN, not about this file's original territory.** Sweep #8
re-cut the thirty territories from the real file structure, so ledger files no longer map one-to-one
onto terminals. T9 of sweep #8 owns `app/kitchen/**`, `app/api/kitchen/[...path]/route.ts` and
`public/panels/kitchen/**`. The sweep rules say a terminal's new rows go in `LEDGER/T<N>.md`, so
they are here — and the ids are globally unique, so nothing above is disturbed.

**Where the kitchen's OLDER rows live: `LEDGER/T6.md`** ("THE KITCHEN SCREEN", 1,500 rows across
three blocks) plus 45 rows in `T10` · `T25` · `T27` · `T28` · `T29` · `T30`. Those were re-run in
this pass and their `result` was updated **in place, in the file where each row lives** — never
copied here.

## What this pass actually did, and what it did not

| | count |
|---|---|
| existing rows re-run **mechanically** and stamped in place | **367** |
| existing rows re-run by **driving the real screen** (headless, port 4309) | **46** |
| existing rows re-checked by **reading a screenshot** | 6 |
| **existing rows NOT re-run — stated honestly below** | ~1,240 |
| **new rows written and executed** | **535** (`P62701`–`P63235`) |
| regressions found | **0** |
| expectation changes recorded | 3 (`P02774`, `P02856`, `P17610`) |
| problems found and fixed | 4 |

**The ~1,240 rows this pass could not re-run, and why.** `T6.md`'s two later blocks —
`P17601`–`P18100` and `P32101`–`P32600`, 1,000 rows — every one of them says *"re-runnable in this
terminal's harness"*, and that harness was T6's own worktree script. It is not in the repo, so
those rows cannot be replayed by anybody; they are indistinguishable from unrun. The remaining
~240 are `T6.md`'s screenshot-judgment rows (`P02876`–`P02925`) and its "a chef, wet hands, twenty
seconds" rows (`P02976`–`P03000`), which are a human read by design. **That is the single biggest
gap in this territory's coverage**, and the fix is the one this pass took for its own rows: put the
assertions in a repo guard, keyed by id, so the next sweep runs them in a second.

## How to re-run any row in this block

```sh
npm run verify:kitchen                        # all 897 mechanical rows, ~1s, no server needed
npm run verify:kitchen -- --only P62701       # exactly one row, forever
node scripts/sweep/t9/live.mjs --base=http://localhost:4309   # the 46 driven rows
```

`scripts/verify-kitchen-screen.mjs` is the guard. **It was sabotage-tested twelve ways** while it
was written — narrow `boardSig` back to a field list · let `tshort` print "Tnull" · a flat 2s poll ·
a silent `return` on a tap · a stale asset hash · drop the 86 drawer's back layer · let the age chip
wrap · silence the settings sheet when a computer prints · shrink the blocked wall · strip a
restaurant filter off a by-id write · point Sign out at the iframe. Eleven of the twelve went red on
the right rows first time. **The twelfth did not**: sabotaging `tshort()` back to the form that
printed the literal "Tnull" in a ticket header left the whole suite green, because no row in any of
the thirty ledgers asserted that guard. It is `P63221`–`P63225` now.

Result key: ✅ pass · ❌ real problem (fixed in this PR) · ⏭ skipped, reason written.

**Owner, 2026-09-03: "can do 7,8,9".** Item 7 = the Ready lane on an iPad in portrait ·
item 8 = the dark-skin status colours · item 9 = a delivery ticket's 🖨 reprint.

**Item 8 was ALREADY BUILT, and the report got that line wrong.** Part 3 of the T9 report carried
T26's old note forward as "STILL OPEN" without checking the token values against today's code. The
kitchen's `--green` / `--amber` / `--red` / `--blue` were matched to the waiter tablet's on
2026-08-22, with the contrast re-measured, and none of the four old warm values (`#5fae6e`,
`#e2664f`, `#d8a657`, `#e6b450`) survives anywhere in the stylesheet. That is exactly the mistake
Part 3 exists to prevent — read the code as it is TODAY, never the old note. So item 8's work is a
GUARD instead of a change: `P63256`–`P63270` pin the kitchen's tokens to the tablet's, in both
skins and across files, so the drift T26 saw cannot come back unnoticed.

**Item 7 needed item 1 first.** At 768px the lanes are 235px and the ticket header wraps; before
item 1 made the age chip `nowrap`, that would have broken the clock at exactly the width this
change enables. Two follow-on fixes to the wrapped header are recorded at `P63301`–`P63312`, along
with the two measured wrong answers, so neither is retried.

# SWEEP #8 · T9 · ROUND 2 — the axis round 1 was weakest on · 500 rows

`P63313`–`P63698` (T9's own block) + `P99801`–`P99915` (claimed) · **500 written, 500 executed, 500 ✅**

**Owner, 2026-09-03: "after making it live and merging plan 500 phases test within your boundaries
make sure it cover everthing within your boundries and test everything again if any error left".**

**Planned from a MEASUREMENT, and the measurement said NOT to do more of the same.** Re-running the
coverage count after round 1 left only **33** named things in this territory unnamed by any check
(it was 69 before round 1). Another pass along that axis would be padding. The real gap is a
different one: **93% of round 1 is STATIC** - it reads the code and confirms a guard is written; it
does not put a value through the guard and look at the pixels.

Every fault this territory's history records is of that shape: `"Tnull"` in a ticket header,
`"496071h 45m"` from a null timestamp, `"NaNh NaNm"`, the VIP badge that read a column which does
not exist, the PARCEL branch that could never run. Each was invisible to a source read and obvious
the moment a value went through. So round 2 is weighted to **driven** checks against the **rendered
DOM**, using the panel's own `state` and its own `render()` - no mocks, nothing re-implemented.

Nothing is written to the database: an injected board never leaves the browser tab, and each block
reloads the real one when it finishes.

**One detector lesson, recorded because it nearly produced 31 fake findings.** The first version
flagged every rendering of `NaN`, `undefined`, `null`, `${x}` and `-->`. All 31 were the panel
faithfully showing data that merely LOOKS like a leak - a dish genuinely called "NaN" must render
as "NaN". A leak only counts when the panel **invented** it: the pattern is in the output and not
in the input. That is what `invented()` in `round2-values.mjs` does, and it is the difference
between a sweep that finds faults and one that manufactures them.

## A - the ticket, driven with hostile values - P63313-P63578

29 values that have historically broken a staff screen (and the ones that obviously could) crossed
with every field the board draws: `table_number` - `kot_no` - `created_at` - dish title - qty -
note - allergies - the table mark - the restaurant's own name - a delivery ticket's source and
customer name - and eleven malformed ORDER shapes. **266 rows, 266 PASS** - the panel invents no
leak from any of them.

## B - the state machine, driven - P63580-P63649

Round 1 asserted `orderPhase()`'s three `return` lines by reading them. This block CROSSES all four
order statuses with 13 dish-status combinations and reads, off the rendered board, the four things a
cook depends on: which lane, which action, which dishes carry a tick, and whether the ticket appears
at all. Plus wall-vs-columns agreement on what is live. **70 rows.**

**It found one thing reading could not: a NEW ticket offered per-dish tick buttons** while telling
the cook "waiting for the waiter to accept". Fixed (`o.status !== "received"` on the tick), and the
reachability is written down rather than assumed - every path that sets a dish to `preparing` is
gated on `.eq("status","received")` and paired with the parent order's accept in all three panel
routes, and `lfh_staff_move_order_item` (mig 175) derives the receiving order's status FROM the
moved dish. So the state is **not reachable through the app today**; it is a contradiction closed,
not a live fault.

## C+F - every overlay at every width in both skins, and the delivery slip's paper - P63650-P63698

Round 1 asserted that each overlay registers a back layer and closes three ways - by reading the
code. This block OPENS all four (86 board · printer sheet · ☰ menu · ⚙️ Settings) on a real screen
at **five widths in two skins** and measures the four things that actually go wrong: does it fit
inside the viewport, is every visible control at least 44px, is any text node clipped, and does the
page scroll sideways while it is open. That is the axis the two "placeholder clipped on the phone
he tests" faults in this repo's history were found on.

**It found a real one: three controls were 41px** - the 86 board's ✕, the printer sheet's ✕ and the
dish-search box - at **every** width, not just a phone. The shared rule already set
`min-height: 44px` and its own comment already said "applies at every width: a phone is touch too";
only the WIDTH was ever set, and the search box got neither. **This is not R41**, which refused a
min-width on `.top-actions .btn` because widening the TOP BAR is exactly what brings back the
four-row bar at 360px - these three live inside overlays where there is no bar to re-wrap, and 3px
moved no layout. Fixed and measured after: 44 × 44.

**F** then reads the PAPER item 9 created - the markup `kotDocHtml` actually returns for a delivery
slip - because item 9 had been verified only by its toast and the flags handed to the document.

## D - every endpoint crossed with every body shape - P99801-P99861

Eleven POST endpoints × (does the branch exist · does every path answer · is every database call
scoped) plus the eighteen body-shape guards, plus the refusal vocabulary: every `err()` is a
sentence, every person-facing refusal is a 4xx, the 403s are only the blocked device and the
platform-accept gate, and every 404 names what was not found.

**Verified by READING the handler, never by calling it with a hostile body.** That is the house
rule - a gap found by reading is REPORTED, not demonstrated - and nothing in this block touches the
network.

## E - cross-panel truth, traced in both directions - P99862-P99915

Round 1's cross-panel rows asserted the CONTRACT (both routes import one print queue, both panels
use one redraw fingerprint). This block traces the other direction: for each thing the kitchen
CHANGES, does every panel that must show it have a path to see it - and does no panel that must
not? Plus the negative half, which matters as much: the kitchen reads no money field, ships none,
shows no owner earnings, writes nothing to `settings`, touches no table another panel owns
exclusively, and has no profile surface in either the panel or `lib/staffProfileShared.ts`.
